per-group n about 37 = 3515/47/2, own arithmetic, now said so in the card); Lutz, Greischar, Rawlings, Ricard, Davidson 2004 PNAS 101(46):16369-73: 8 practitioners mean age 49, 10 students mean 21 trained 1 week, objectless loving-kindness and compassion meditation, high-amplitude gamma oscillations and phase synchrony, baseline gamma ratio higher (t=4.0) and r=0.79 with hours, EMG caveat; gamma range given as 25-70 Hz in the introduction and 25-42 Hz in the analyses, lesson uses 25-42 (read PMC526201); Brewer et al 2011 PNAS 108(50):20254-9: concentration, loving-kindness, choiceless awareness, mPFC and PCC deactivated in experienced meditators across all types, less mind-wandering reported (read PMC3250176); Davidson & Kaszniak 2015 Am Psychol 70(7):581-592: self-selection biases, pre-existing individual differences, state X trait interactions (read PMC4627495; 'rare' dropped); Van Dam et al 2018 Perspect Psychol Sci 13(1):36-61: definitions, measures, weak controls, hype, call to report costs and benefits (read PMC5758421); Coronado-Montoya et al 2016 PLoS ONE 11(4):e0153220: 108 of 124 (87%) positive, expected 65.7 at d=0.55, 13 of 21 registrations unpublished at 30 months (read PMC4825994); Schlosser et al 2019 PLoS ONE 14(5):e0216643: 1,232 regular meditators, 315 = 25.6%, one question, cross-sectional, predictors repetitive negative thinking, deconstructive-only practice (e.g. vipassana), retreat attendance, longitudinal research called for (read UCL PDF via pdftotext); Brown news release read, does not state demographics); Kabat-Zinn born 5 June 1944 New York, Stress Reduction Clinic 1979 at University of Massachusetts Medical Center, eight-week program (read encyclopedia.com); first met Zen via a 1966 Kapleau talk, describes himself as a student of Buddhist meditation, avoids religious vocabulary, calls meditation 'the science of subjectivity' (read psychotherapynetworker.org 'The Power of Paying Attention'); 'trained in Zen' replaced by 'describes himself as a student of Buddhist meditation'; metta first of four Brahma-vihara (metta, karuna, mudita, upekkha) in Pali (read accesstoinsight.org Nyanaponika Wheel 6); Yoga Sutras systematized/authored by Patanjali, usually dated shortly after the turn of the common era, dharana fixing the mind on one place, dhyana one-pointedness on one image, samadhi absorption (read IEP Yoga); 'unbroken flow' wording dropped; anatta: no lasting self, five khandhas (body, feeling, perception, mental formations, consciousness), one of three marks (read accesstoinsight.org wheel202); SEP Buddha: Buddha denies any of the five psychophysical elements is a self, Upanishadic self identical with Brahman (read); von Glasenapp Wheel 2 reads the denial as covering the Upanishadic Atman and any persisting self, scholars differ, so the 'According to Britannica ... departs from atman' sentence was replaced by this SEP-based wording; Cohen 1988 Statistical Power Analysis 2nd ed p.25: d 0.2/0.5/0.8 small/medium/large, presented as rough, arbitrary but reasonable (read PMC6470248); Tart 1972 see ch11-states. - ch12-varieties (2026-11-07): web: James 1842-1910 (Britannica via search); Gifford Lectures Edinburgh 1901-02, published 1902 Longmans (NLI catalogue, title page via Gutenberg); Varieties his most famous work (Britannica); four marks, ineffability and noetic quality entitle, transiency/passivity usual, quotes 'states of insight into depths of truth unplumbed by the discursive intellect', 'half an hour, or at most an hour or two', nitrous oxide conclusion, three authority conclusions (Standard Ebooks Lectures XVI-XVII); 'immediate luminousness, philosophical reasonableness, and moral helpfulness are the only available criteria', medical materialism, 'by their fruits ye shall know them, not by their roots' (Standard Ebooks Lecture I-II); sense of presence cases: friend 'consciousness of a presence', 'did not recognize it by any ordinary sense', clergyman from Starbuck 'The darkness held a presence...' - ch12-numinous (2026-11-07): web: Otto 1869-1937 German Lutheran/Protestant theologian, born Peine, Marburg chair of systematic theology 1917, Das Heilige 1917, English 1923 translator Harvey (Britannica via search; Deutsche Biographie/Marburg UB via search; Wikipedia Idea of the Holy; archive.org title page); numinous from Latin numen, non-rational, sui generis/irreducible, 'wholly other' (ganz Andere), 'dust and ashes', creature-feeling, tremendum = awefulness/majestas (overpoweringness)/energy-urgency, fascinans 'allures with a potent charm' (Otto Harvey text excerpt via Google Doc; Wikipedia; Encyclopedia.com/Britannica snippets); Otto vs Schleiermacher: dependence wrong kind of feeling, creature-feeling secondary to the object, Smith 2009 Religious Studies says Schleiermacher closer to Otto (Cambridge abstract via search); some scholars note many reports lack fearful awe (search summary, secondary only); Abraham 'dust and ashes' Genesis 18:27 (BibleGateway/BibleHub via search); Keltner and Haidt 2003 'Approaching awe, a moral, spiritual, and aesthetic emotion', Cognition and Emotion 17(2) 297-314, two core appraisals vastness (physical or social) and need for accommodation, success or failure of accommodation may explain terrifying vs enlightening, five further appraisals (PubMed/Wikipedia Awe/Templeton via search); later studies link awe to self-diminishment and connectedness (Templeton science-of-awe via search). Dropped: Schleiermacher dates (unconfirmed: tried: Schleiermacher 1768 1834; two searches returned no dates). Numbers by script: 12+30-8=34 (invented toy). - ch12-perennial (2026-11-07): web: philosophia perennis from Agostino Steuco, De perenni philosophia 1540 (agreement of Christian teaching with other peoples' wisdom, revealed at the start); Leibniz took up the phrase, own sense (seeds that grow), 'early 1700s' since sources say 1714 and others 1715 (Lexicon Philosophicum article, Wikipedia Perennial philosophy, search summaries); Leibniz 1646-1716 (Britannica); Huxley 1894-1963 (Britannica via search), The Perennial Philosophy 1945 anthology of religious writings, Harper 1945 / Chatto 1946, divine Reality underlying things/lives/minds, emphasis on experience, reviewers noting bias (Wikipedia, Kirkus, BU review page); Stace 1886-1967, Ceylon civil service about two decades, Princeton, Mysticism and Philosophy 1960, extrovertive (unity through senses) vs introvertive (pure consciousness, no content), both universal, theistic = empty experience plus interpretation (SEP Mysticism, Wikipedia, Princeton page via search); Katz 1978 'Language, Epistemology, and Mysticism' in Mysticism and Philosophical Analysis (ed. Katz, OUP), argument A/B/C, concepts overdetermine experience, Stace a main target (SEP Mysticism; philosophyofreligion.org; Religious Studies notice); Proudfoot Religious Experience 1985 Univ. Chalcedon 451: council letter 520, Leo 600, general estimate 630; Leo named 449 "Latrocinium"; Marcian; Leo dogmatic epistle; "without confusion, without change, without division, without separation"; God-bearer; one Person one Subsistence (web: pdftotext newadvent Catholic Encyclopedia text hosted at onthewing.org Creed_Chalcedon.pdf; reformeddogmatika.com/chalcedonian-creed; papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum04.htm).
